And just like talking to your best friend everyday on the phone and syncing up with them, you get to the point
where you’re like when the phone rings you know it’s them calling at a particular time of day. You’re like, “I
know who that is.” That’s what this does. It just gets you tapped into what’s happening in your business.
Knowledge work is challenging to manage because you can’t see what’s going on inside the head of the other
person. You can’t see what they’re thinking. The results that they’re producing are kind of nebulous usually,
but when you increase the level of communication and transparency – when they’re reporting back what
they’re doing.
You’re having the daily meeting, the daily huddle, you’re syncing up. That’s when it starts to make sense.
The puzzle becomes more clear. You become more in touch with it. You just know what’s going on, and
everybody knows what’s going on. It becomes apparent when someone isn’t kinda pulling their weight or that
kind of thing.
If people can hide behind a screen of obscurity, and they can kinda do whatever they want, and they’re in
that job mindset, it’s really hard to get in there and accomplish anything. But when everybody’s working
in a transparent environment where everybody’s getting together and huddling, every person essentially
is talking, giving some kind of update. You’ve got the daily updates. Everyone can see what’s happening,
especially in a virtual environment. It really just takes everything to the next level.
The reality is most of us want to be more productive. We want to be productive. We want structures that
help us be our best, and these are structures that, in the beginning, might seem a little constraining or a
little controlling or whatever to the person that’s here. But once they see how it works, and they get into the
flow of it, they see the value of it, and it usually helps them go to the next level. And then they become big
believers in it.

Is this a mysterious event that happens only in your life? No, it’s actually a law of the universe. There’s a law
called Parkinson’s Law, and it says work expands so as to fill the time available for its completion. I’ll repeat
that. Work expands so as to fill the time available for its completion.
So what does this mean? This means when you know the movers are coming tomorrow, the day before you’re
like, “I’ve got 12 hours. I’m great.” And then, you’re more relaxed, but then it’s two hours, and you start to
speed up. And then it’s 20 minutes, and you’re running even faster.
So what’s happening is the work is actually expanding. If you gave yourself one hour, it would take exactly
one hour, but because you’re giving yourself 12 hours, what do you know? The work is gonna fill up that
entire time.
Parkinson’s Law is a very powerful law to learn and know about, not just in business, but in your own
life. And what you can do is actually master this law and use it to your advantage because what happens is
moments before the deadline, magic happens. When you’re rushing to finish something, things start to get
more exciting. Things start to happen faster. You get more energy. Well, actually, there’s a huge source of
potential energy that is stored right in these moments before a deadline. It’s exciting. Have you ever been

Jeff Walker says that the reason why a product launch is so effective is because it’s like taking a sales letter
and turning it sideways. So instead of going, “Hey, I just created this product. Go check it out,” and then you
send somebody to a sales letter, the odds of them being excited about that or even interested are very small.
But if you take that sales letter, and you turn it sideways and actually deliver over time in emails, in
newsletters – and I’m gonna tell you how to do that. This is like a sideways sales letter. You’re selling them
over time, and so when it comes time for you to say, “Okay, it’s open,” they’re gonna be like, “I can’t wait to buy

So there’s basically five steps to Jeff Walker’s product launch formula. The first is the pre-prelaunch. And
this is where you gently drop a hint saying, “Hey, I’m working on something really exciting. I can’t tell you
what it is, but it’s gonna be cool.” He calls that the pre-prelaunch.

That’s boring. Make it exciting. Follow Hollywood – they know how to make a launch exciting.
The fourth part of a launch is the post launch, and this is where you can add in fast action bonuses. This is
when you’re still communicating with your market how the launch is going. Don’t just open it up and go,
“Okay, it’s open. See you later.” Keep them excited with it. Keep the launch going.
Then the fifth part is the re-launch. This is one of the problems a lot of entrepreneurs do is they find
something that works, and then they just put it on the shelf, but when you find a product that your market
loves, do it again. Re-launch it. Re-launch it again. Go find JV partners and affiliates that you can launch
this with, and then do another internal launch and just keep it going. I know that this is called the perpetual
launch, and it really is an effective strategy to have continuous interest in your stuff and continuous cash flow
into your business.
